│             │ │ performance regression in the MoE (Mixture of  │
│             │ │ Experts) align sum kernels. The problem was in │
│             │ │ the `moe_sum` function in                      │
│             │ │ `csrc/moe/moe_align_sum_kernels.cu`.\n\n###    │
│             │ │ Root Cause\n\nThe `moe_sum` function had       │
│             │ │ optimized CUDA kernels for specific topk       │
│             │ │ values (2, 3, 4) but fell back to a generic    │
│             │ │ PyTorch operation (`at::sum_out`) for other    │
│             │ │ topk values. This fallback was significantly   │
│             │ │ slower than a custom CUDA                      │
│             │ │ kernel.\n\n```cpp\n// Before: Only optimized   │
│             │ │ for topk=2,3,4\nswitch (topk) {\n  case 2: /*  │
│             │ │ optimized kernel */ break;\n  case 3: /*       │
│             │ │ optimized kernel */ break;\n  case 4: /*       │
│             │ │ optimized kernel */ break;\n  default:         │
│             │ │ at::sum_out(output, input, 1);  // SLOW        │
│             │ │ fallback!\n}\n```\n\n## Solution\n\nAdded a    │
│             │ │ generic CUDA kernel that works for any topk    │
│             │ │ value, maintaining the same optimization       │
│             │ │ patterns as the specialized kernels.\n\n###    │
│             │ │ Changes Made\n\n**File:                        │
│             │ │ `csrc/moe/moe_align_sum_kernels.cu`**\n\n1.    │
│             │ │ **Added generic kernel** (lines                │
│             │ │ 214-228):\n```cpp\ntemplate <typename          │
│             │ │ scalar_t>\n__global__ void moe_sum_kernel(\n   │
│             │ │ scalar_t* __restrict__ out,          // [...,  │
│             │ │ d]\n    const scalar_t* __restrict__ input,    │
│             │ │ // [..., topk, d]\n    const int d,\n    const │
│             │ │ int topk) {\n  const int64_t token_idx =       │
│             │ │ blockIdx.x;\n  for (int64_t idx = threadIdx.x; │
│             │ │ idx < d; idx += blockDim.x) {\n    scalar_t x  │
│             │ │ = 0.0;\n    for (int k = 0; k < topk; ++k) {\n │
│             │ │ x += VLLM_LDG(&input);\n    }\n    out = x;\n  │
│             │ │ }\n}\n```\n\n2. **Updated moe_sum function**   │
│             │ │ (lines 359-365):\n```cpp\ndefault:\n           │
│             │ │ VLLM_DISPATCH_FLOATING_TYPES(input.scalar_typ… │
│             │ │ "moe_sum_kernel", [&] {\n                      │
│             │ │ vllm::moe::moe_sum_kernel<scalar_t><<<grid,    │
│             │ │ block, 0, stream>>>(\n                         │
│             │ │ output.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),                   │
│             │ │ input.data_ptr<scalar_t>(),\n                  │
│             │ │ hidden_size, topk);\n  });\n                   │
│             │ │ break;\n```\n\n### Key Features of the Generic │
│             │ │ Kernel\n\n1. **Same structure as specialized   │
│             │ │ kernels**: Uses the same grid/block            │
│             │ │ configuration and memory access patterns\n2.   │
│             │ │ **Optimized memory access**: Uses `VLLM_LDG`   │
│             │ │ for coalesced memory reads\n3. **No template   │
│             │ │ parameter**: Uses runtime `topk` parameter     │
│             │ │ instead of compile-time constant\n4.           │
│             │ │ **Efficient thread utilization**: Each thread  │
│             │ │ processes multiple elements using stride       │
│             │ │ loops\n\n## Performance Impact\n\n###          │
│             │ │ Before\n- topk=2,3,4: Fast (optimized CUDA     │
│             │ │ kernel)\n- topk=6,8,etc.: Slow (PyTorch        │
│             │ │ fallback)\n\n### After\n- topk=2,3,4: Fast     │
│             │ │ (optimized CUDA kernel with `#pragma           │
│             │ │ unroll`)\n- topk=6,8,etc.: Fast (custom CUDA   │
│             │ │ kernel)\n\n### Expected Speedup\nFor MoE       │
│             │ │ models with topk > 4 (e.g., DeepSeek-V3 with   │
│             │ │ topk=8), the performance improvement should be │
│             │ │ significant:\n- Elimination of PyTorch         │
│             │ │ overhead\n- Better memory access patterns\n-   │
│             │ │ Optimized GPU utilization\n\n##                │
│             │ │ Verification\n\n### Code Verification\n- ✅    │
│             │ │ Generic kernel is present\n- ✅ Default case   │
│             │ │ uses generic kernel\n- ✅ Old fallback to      │
│             │ │ `at::sum_out` is removed\n- ✅ Specialized     │
│             │ │ kernels (topk=2,3,4) still present\n\n###      │
│             │ │ Correctness Testing\n- ✅ Shape tests passed   │
│             │ │ for all topk values (2,3,4,6,8)\n- ✅ Shape    │
│             │ │ tests passed for different dtypes (float16,    │
│             │ │ float32, bfloat16)\n- ✅ Shape tests passed    │
│             │ │ for different tensor shapes\n\n## Technical    │
│             │ │ Details\n\n### Kernel Configuration\n- **Grid  │
│             │ │ size**: `num_tokens` (one block per token)\n-  │
│             │ │ **Block size**: `min(hidden_size, 1024)` (up   │
│             │ │ to 1024 threads)\n- **Memory access**:         │
│             │ │ Coalesced reads using `VLLM_LDG`\n- **Thread   │
│             │ │ utilization**: Each thread processes           │
│             │ │ `hidden_size / block_size` elements\n\n###     │
│             │ │ Comparison with Specialized Kernels\nThe       │
│             │ │ generic kernel follows the same pattern as     │
│             │ │ specialized kernels:\n- Same grid/block        │
│             │ │ configuration\n- Same memory access            │
│             │ │ patterns\n- Same thread utilization            │
│             │ │ strategy\n- Only difference: loop bound is     │
│             │ │ runtime parameter instead of compile-time      │
│             │ │ constant\n\n## Files Modified\n\n-             │
│             │ │ `csrc/moe/moe_align_sum_kernels.cu`: Added     │
│             │ │ generic kernel and updated moe_sum             │
│             │ │ function\n\n## Testing\n\nRun the verification │
│             │ │ script:\n```bash\npython                       │
│             │ │ .bench_scratch/verify_changes.py\n```\n\nRun   │
│             │ │ the correctness test:\n```bash\npython         │
│             │ │ .bench_scratch/test_moe_sum_correctness.py\n`… │
│             │ │ Notes\n\n1. The specialized kernels for        │
│             │ │ topk=2,3,4 still use `#pragma unroll` for      │
│             │ │ maximum performance\n2. The generic kernel is  │
│             │ │ slightly less optimized (no unrolling) but     │
│             │ │ much faster than PyTorch fallback\n3. For      │
│             │ │ future optimization, we could add more         │
│             │ │ specialized kernels for common topk values     │
│             │ │ (e.g., 6, 8)\n4. The implementation maintains  │
│             │ │ backward compatibility - all existing code     │
│             │ │ continues to work\n\n## References\n\n- Issue  │
│             │ │ #12253: Performance regression in MoE align    │
│             │ │ sum kernels\n- Commit #12380: Fix perf         │
│             │ │ regression from #12253\n- Original kernel      │
│             │ │ implementation:                                │
│             │ │ https://github.com/sgl-project/sglang/commit/… │
│             │ │ 'old_str': '# MoE Align Sum Kernels            │
